### 🤖 Claude · 17:25:14

Done — the signing config is fully wired and verified. Here's what landed:

## Signing setup (all in `desktop/`)

| File | Role |
|---|---|
| `build/entitlements.mac.plist` | Hardened-runtime entitlements Electron needs for notarization |
| `build/notarize.cjs` | afterSign hook — notarizes via `notarytool`, **skips cleanly if Apple creds absent** |
| `package.json` (build) | Dropped `identity: null`; added `hardenedRuntime`, `entitlements`, `gatekeeperAssess: false`, `afterSign`; added `@electron/notarize` dep |
| `.github/workflows/desktop-build.yml` | Per-OS build steps that read signing secrets — signs when set, unsigned otherwise |

## How it behaves

- **No secrets** → builds **unsigned** (verified just now: notarize hook logged *"skipped"*, still produced a valid 98 MB `.dmg`, no crash). CI won't break.
- **Secrets present** → electron-builder signs with your cert and the hook notarizes — a clean, no-warning installer.

## To turn signing on, add these repo secrets (Settings → Secrets and variables → Actions)

**macOS** (needs a **paid** Apple Developer account — the free ID you're using for the phone won't issue a Developer ID):
`MAC_CSC_LINK` (base64 of your *Developer ID Application* `.p12`), `MAC_CSC_KEY_PASSWORD`, `APPLE_ID`, `APPLE_APP_SPECIFIC_PASSWORD`, `APPLE_TEAM_ID`.

**Windows** (Authenticode cert): `WIN_CSC_LINK` (base64 of `.pfx`), `WIN_CSC_KEY_PASSWORD`.

> Make the base64 with `base64 -i cert.p12 | pbcopy`. Full walkthrough + how to export the cert is in **`desktop/README.md` → Code signing**.

Both READMEs are updated to reflect this. Everything's committed to `main` and the workflow YAML validates.

**Bottom line:** the pipeline is signing-*ready* today. Until you add the paid Apple account + certs, installers keep building unsigned (with the documented Gatekeeper bypass) — the moment you drop the secrets in, the same workflow produces signed, notarized `.dmg`/`.exe` with zero further changes.

### 👤 User · 17:28:56

Can I close terminal window once app is installed on my phone and ipad, and synced

### 🤖 Claude · 17:29:48

Short answer: **not with the build you have now.** The dev build you installed (`expo run:ios`) loads its JavaScript from **Metro** — the server running in your terminal. Close the terminal and the app can't launch/reload (you'll get a red *"can't connect to development server"* on next open). An already-open app keeps working until you quit it, but that's it.

There are actually **two separate things running** that matter — don't conflate them:

| What's running | Needed for | How to cut the cord |
|---|---|---|
| **Metro** (the terminal) | Only a **dev build** to load its JS | Build a **Release** version — JS gets bundled *into* the app, no Metro |
| **Desktop host** (streamPhoneDeck) | The phone to control the Mac **at all** | Run the **installed app** (from the `.dmg`) with **Open at login**, not `npm start` |

## To go fully terminal-free

1. **Rebuild the phone + iPad app in Release** (embeds the JS — no Metro):
   ```bash
   npx expo run:ios --device --configuration Release
   ```
   Run it once per device. *(On your free Apple ID it still expires in ~7 days and needs a rebuild; a paid account or `eas build --profile production` removes that.)*
2. **Run the desktop host as the installed app** — install the `.dmg`, launch it, and enable **Open at login** from its tray menu. Then no terminal is involved, and it starts with your Mac.
3. Keep the phone/iPad **on the same Wi-Fi** as the Mac.

## About "synced" — a heads-up

The phone and iPad **aren't synced**. Each device **pairs separately** (its own key) and keeps **its own deck layout** (stored on-device). So:
- Pair each one individually (scan the QR on each).
- Edits you make to the deck on the phone **won't** appear on the iPad, and vice-versa.

There's no cross-device sync built in — that'd need a server-backed store (a possible future addition). Want me to add per-device… actually, want me to sketch how a shared/synced deck across your devices would work, or set up the Release build so you can ditch the terminal?

That `main.jsbundle` being written *into* the app is exactly what frees you from Metro. It's now compiling + installing (a few more minutes); I'll confirm when it lands.

## What I set up
- Added **`npm run ios:release`** to `mobile/package.json` — re-run it anytime (free-account builds expire ~every 7 days, and it's how you'll build for the iPad). It prompts you to pick the device.

## Once the build finishes
- You can **close the terminal / stop Metro** — the phone app now runs on its own.
- **For the iPad:** plug it in, then `cd mobile && npm run ios:release` → pick the iPad from the list.

## One thing that still has to run (not the terminal)
The app can only control your Mac while the **desktop host** is running. So the last step to be truly hands-off:
- Install the host from the **`.dmg`** (or the CI artifact), launch it once, and turn on **Open at login** from its tray menu.
- Then the host starts with your Mac automatically — no terminal, no `npm start`.

With the Release app on the devices **and** the host set to open at login, you're fully terminal-free (both just need to be on the same Wi-Fi).

I'll report back the moment the iPhone install completes — then you can close that terminal.

## For the iPad (do this *after* the iPhone build finishes)

   ```bash
   cd mobile && npm run ios:release
   ```
   Pick **iPad (3)** from the device list. *(The command you ran was `expo run:ios --device`, which builds Debug and stays tethered to Metro — `ios:release` is the standalone one.)*
3. First launch → **Trust** the developer cert: iPad → Settings → General → VPN & Device Management → your Apple ID → Trust.

So: wait for the iPhone build to land (I'll tell you), then enable Developer Mode on the iPad and run `npm run ios:release`. I'll report the moment the iPhone install completes.

### 🤖 Claude · 17:40:15

Still timing out — but now it's **purely a device-connection issue**, not the build. `devicectl` error 60 means it can't complete its tunnel to the iPhone, and the #1 cause is the **iPhone being locked/asleep** (or connected over Wi-Fi instead of USB). The Release app is built and ready; it just needs a stable connection to land.

Please do this on the iPhone / Mac, then I'll retry the install (no rebuild — it's instant):

1. **Unlock the iPhone and keep the screen on** (don't let it sleep during install). This alone fixes error 60 most of the time.
2. Make sure it's on a **USB cable** and trusted (you tapped Trust earlier).
3. In Xcode → **Window → Devices and Simulators** → select **SDphone** → **uncheck "Connect via network"** (forces the reliable USB path instead of a Wi-Fi tunnel that times out).

Then reply **"go"** and I'll re-run the direct install of the already-built Release app.

If it still refuses, the robust fallback is to install from the Xcode GUI:
> `open mobile/ios/streamPhoneDeck.xcworkspace` → pick **SDphone** as the run target (top bar) → press **▶ Run**. Xcode's installer is more forgiving than the CLI, and it'll also register the device.

Once the Release app is on the iPhone and you launch it once, **you can close the terminal** — it runs standalone from then on.

```diff
- export type DeckButton = {

+ export type DeckButton = {

  icon: string;
  action: ButtonAction;
  color: string; // a hex from TILE_COLORS
  w?: number; // column span (default 1)
  h?: number; // row span (default 1)
};

/** Grid layout limits (columns = "layout size", span = per-tile resize). */
export const MIN_COLS = 2;
export const MAX_COLS = 5;
export const DEFAULT_COLS = 3;
export const MAX_SPAN = 4;

/** Clamp a stored span to a sane integer ≥ 1. */
export function clampSpan(v: unknown): number {
  const n = typeof v === "number" ? Math.round(v) : 1;
  return Math.max(1, Math.min(n, MAX_SPAN));
}
```

```
// Grid packing for the deck. Tiles can span multiple columns/rows (w×h); this
// places them first-fit into a `cols`-wide grid, scanning row by row for the
// first spot each tile fits. Returns absolute cell coordinates + the total row
// count so the renderer can position tiles and size the container.

import { clampSpan, type DeckButton } from "./buttons";

export type Placement = { tile: DeckButton; x: number; y: number; w: number; h: number };

export function packDeck(tiles: DeckButton[], cols: number): { placements: Placement[]; rows: number } {
  const occupied: boolean[][] = []; // occupied[row][col]

  const rowOf = (r: number) => (occupied[r] ??= new Array(cols).fill(false));

  const fits = (x: number, y: number, w: number, h: number): boolean => {
    if (x + w > cols) return false;
    for (let r = y; r < y + h; r++) {
      const row = occupied[r];
      if (!row) continue; // empty row → free
      for (let c = x; c < x + w; c++) if (row[c]) return false;
    }
    return true;
  };

  const fill = (x: number, y: number, w: number, h: number) => {
    for (let r = y; r < y + h; r++) {
      const row = rowOf(r);
      for (let c = x; c < x + w; c++) row[c] = true;
    }
  };

  const placements: Placement[] = [];
  for (const tile of tiles) {
    const w = Math.min(clampSpan(tile.w), cols); // never wider than the grid
    const h = clampSpan(tile.h);
    let placed = false;
    for (let y = 0; !placed; y++) {
      for (let x = 0; x + w <= cols; x++) {
      
… [truncated, 223 more chars]
```

```diff
- import AsyncStorage from "@react-native-async-storage/async-storage";
import { defaultButtons, newButtonId, validateAction, type DeckButton } from "./buttons";

const DECK_KEY = "streamphonedeck.deck.v1";
+ import AsyncStorage from "@react-native-async-storage/async-storage";
import {
  clampSpan,
  DEFAULT_COLS,
  MAX_COLS,
  MIN_COLS,
  defaultButtons,
  newButtonId,
  validateAction,
  type DeckButton,
} from "./buttons";

const DECK_KEY = "streamphonedeck.deck.v1";
const COLS_KEY = "streamphonedeck.cols";

/** Layout size = number of grid columns. */
export async function loadCols(): Promise<number> {
  const raw = await AsyncStorage.getItem(COLS_KEY);
  const n = raw ? parseInt(raw, 10) : DEFAULT_COLS;
  return Number.isFinite(n) ? Math.max(MIN_COLS, Math.min(n, MAX_COLS)) : DEFAULT_COLS;
}

export async function saveCols(cols: number): Promise<void> {
  await AsyncStorage.setItem(COLS_KEY, String(Math.max(MIN_COLS, Math.min(cols, MAX_COLS))));
}
```

### 🤖 Claude · 23:14:47

tsc clean. Let me sanity-check the packing algorithm itself (the one piece of non-trivial logic) — verify a mixed layout packs with no overlaps and stays in-bounds:

**🔧 Bash**
*Verify grid packing has no overlaps*
```bash
node -e '
const clampSpan = v => Math.max(1, Math.min(typeof v==="number"?Math.round(v):1, 4));
function packDeck(tiles, cols){
  const occ=[]; const rowOf=r=>(occ[r]??=new Array(cols).fill(false));
  const fits=(x,y,w,h)=>{ if(x+w>cols)return false; for(let r=y;r<y+h;r++){const row=occ[r]; if(!row)continue; for(let c=x;c<x+w;c++) if(row[c])return false;} return true; };
  const fill=(x,y,w,h)=>{ for(let r=y;r<y+h;r++){const row=rowOf(r); for(let c=x;c<x+w;c++) row[c]=true;} };
  const placements=[];
  for(const t of tiles){ const w=Math.min(clampSpan(t.w),cols); const h=clampSpan(t.h); let placed=false; for(let y=0;!placed;y++){ for(let x=0;x+w<=cols;x++){ if(fits(x,y,w,h)){ fill(x,y,w,h); placements.push({x,y,w,h}); placed=true; break; } } } }
  return { placements, rows: occ.length };
}
const tiles=[{w:2,h:2},{},{},{},{},{w:3,h:1}];  // 2x2, four 1x1, a full-width 3x1
const {placements,rows}=packDeck(tiles,3);
const grid={}; let ok=true;
for(const p of placements){ if(p.x+p.w>3){ok=false;console.log("OUT-OF-BOUNDS",p);} for(let r=p.y;r<p.y+p.h;r++)for(let c=p.x;c<p.x+p.w;c++){const k=r+","+c; if(grid[k]){ok=false;console.log("OVERLAP",k,p);} grid[k]=true;} }
console.log("placements:", JSON.stringify(placements));
console.log("rows:", rows, "| overlap-free & in-bounds:", ok);
process.exit(ok?0:1);
'
```

```
rows: 4 | overlap-free & in-bounds: true
```

### 🤖 Claude · 23:15:46

Done — the deck is now a **fully resizable grid**. Here's what shipped:

## What you can do now (in Edit mode)

- **Set the layout size** — a **Layout: 2 / 3 / 4 / 5 wide** control changes the number of columns (tiles resize to fit). Persists per device.
- **Resize individual tiles** — each tile's editor has a new **Size (grid cells)** picker: **Width** (1…columns) and **Height** (1…4). So you can make a button 2×1, 2×2, a full-width banner, etc.
- Tiles **auto-pack** first-fit into the grid, flowing around the bigger ones.

## How it's built

| File | Change |
|---|---|
| `src/buttons.ts` | `w`/`h` span fields + grid limits (`MIN/MAX_COLS`, `MAX_SPAN`) + `clampSpan` |
| `src/layout.ts` (new) | `packDeck()` — first-fit placement of w×h tiles into N columns |
| `src/deckStorage.ts` | persists column count + coerces spans on load |
| `App.tsx` | absolute-positioned packed grid (cell size from measured width) + the Layout control |
| `src/TileEditor.tsx` | the Width/Height size picker |

## Verified
- ✅ `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- ✅ Packing logic checked headlessly — a 2×2 + four 1×1 + a full-width tile pack with **no overlaps, all in-bounds**, correct row count.

## ⚠️ One catch — you'll need to rebuild the devices

Your iPhone/iPad are running the **Release** build (JS embedded), which **doesn't hot-reload**. To get this feature onto them, rebuild:
```bash
cd mobile && npm run ios:release   # pick the device
```
(If you still have a **simulator** dev build with Metro running, it Fast-Refreshes automatically — good for eyeballing it first.)

Everything's committed to `main`. Want me to rebuild + install the updated Release onto the iPad (and iPhone) now, or fire up the simulator so you can preview the resizable grid first?

### 🤖 Claude · 00:20:20

The site describes the *old* web-deck architecture — it needs a real content update (the native app + desktop host + encrypted pairing). Big scope overall, so let me work through it: Android workflow → verify everything → website → README → trigger the Android build.

First, upgrade the Android CI to build a **standalone Release APK** (embedded JS, runs without a computer) with optional keystore signing:

**🔧 Write**
`/Users/shubhamarundekatey/ClaudeCode/day27/streamPhoneDeck/.github/workflows/mobile-android.yml`
```
name: mobile-android-build

# Builds a STANDALONE Android APK (release: JS embedded, runs without Metro) and
# uploads it as an artifact you can download and sideload. By default it's signed
# with Expo's template debug key (installable + stable signature for testing). Add
# a real keystore via secrets to sign for wider distribution / the Play Store:
#   ANDROID_KEYSTORE_BASE64   base64 of your release .jks/.keystore
#   ANDROID_KEYSTORE_PASSWORD / ANDROID_KEY_ALIAS / ANDROID_KEY_PASSWORD
# Make base64:  base64 -i release.keystore | pbcopy

on:
  workflow_dispatch:
  push:
    branches: [main]
    paths:
      - "mobile/**"
      - ".github/workflows/mobile-android.yml"

jobs:
  build:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    defaults:
      run:
        working-directory: mobile
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4

      - uses: actions/setup-node@v4
        with:
          node-version: 20
          cache: npm
          cache-dependency-path: mobile/package-lock.json

      - uses: actions/setup-java@v4
        with:
          distribution: temurin
          java-version: 17

      - name: Install dependencies
        run: npm ci

      - name: Prebuild native Android project
        run: npx expo prebuild --platform android --no-install

      - name: Decode release keystore (if configured)
        id: keystore
        if: ${{ secrets.ANDROID_KEYSTORE_BASE64 != '' }}
        run: |
          echo "${{ secrets.ANDROID_KEYSTORE_BASE64 }}" | base64 --decode > "$RUNNER_TEMP/rel
… [truncated, 953 more chars]
```

### 🤖 Claude · 00:29:50

A real bug in the workflow — GitHub **doesn't allow the `secrets` context in `if:` conditions**. That's why the recent runs failed instantly (0s). The fix: read secrets via `env` (which *does* allow them) and branch in the shell. Rewriting the build step:

### 🤖 Claude · 00:46:24

All three asks are done and verified.

## 1. Refined everything (verification pass — all green)
- **Mobile:** `tsc` clean · AES-GCM interop ✓ · X25519 handshake interop ✓
- **Desktop:** host protocol test-client — all 8 spd2 checks (forgery / tamper / replay rejected, action executes) ✓
- **Workflows:** all three YAML files parse ✓
- **Fixes found & applied:** added a configurable **`DECK_PORT`** to the host CLI; and caught a **broken Android workflow** — it referenced `secrets` inside an `if:` (which GitHub forbids), so every run was failing in 0s. Reworked it to read secrets via `env` and branch in the shell.

## 2. Website (`site/index.html`) — rewritten to match reality
- Hero + "how it works" now describe the **native app → encrypted → desktop host → OS** path (was the old web-relay/agent story).
- Actions: **5 cards** now (added **Type text**); `keys` noted macOS & Windows.
- Editing: added **resize the grid & tiles** + a Width selector in the mock.
- "Get running": the **install-host → run-app → scan-QR** flow (not `npm run dev`/agent).
- Security section **flipped** from "no auth, be careful" to **"secure by design"** (X25519, AES-256-GCM, forward secrecy, per-device revocable keys) with one honest `shell` caveat.
- Validated: all nav anchors resolve, 5 sections balanced, HTML parses clean.

## 3. READMEs — refined
Resizable grid / layout size, Android **standalone release APK**, `site/` in the repo map, and the pair-&-use steps.

## 4. Android build — done in CI ✅
Upgraded `mobile-android.yml` to build a **standalone release APK** (JS embedded → runs without Metro), signing-ready via optional keystore secrets. Triggered it, watched it succeed (~13 min), and **verified the artifact**: `app-release.apk`, **90 MB**, containing `classes.dex`, `AndroidManifest.xml`, and — the key part — **`assets/index.android.bundle`** (embedded JS = truly standalone).

**Grab it:**
```
gh run download 30604348736 -n streamphonedeck-android
```
or GitHub → Actions → *mobile-android-build* → Artifacts → **streamphonedeck-android**. Sideload on any Android device (allow "install unknown apps"), open it, scan the QR — no computer tether needed.

> Note: it's **debug-signed** by default (stable signature, installs fine for testing). For Play Store distribution, add the `ANDROID_KEYSTORE_*` secrets (documented in the workflow header) and it signs with your real key.

Everything's committed and pushed to `main`. Want me to also kick off the **desktop-build** (`.dmg`/`.exe`) run so you have fresh installers alongside the APK?
